Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

Hard-block primitive to instantiate: `mac_int_9x9`   (precision INT9)
  computes: acc += a*b
  input ports:  clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  output ports: out[18]
  arithmetic: the block's operands and result are unsigned. A design operand narrower than its port must be zero-extended to the port width. A signed design operand has no exact representation on this block. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  timing: `out` is registered inside the block on `clk`; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
  registers: the block registers its operands (`a`, `b`) at one clock edge and `out` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in `out` after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `out` is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and `reset` is its only control (no enable, no load).
  reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low; it is the only control of the running sum (no enable, no load), and every register is unknown until that first clear.
  power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by `reset`; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  Wire EVERY operand of the fused region onto the input ports (name them exactly as above); drive the region's real consumer from the output port. Tie unused term ports to 0. Do not leave input ports unconnected.

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, seed 1) has a critical path of 6.06654 ns and uses 88 DSP tiles of the device's 132, four blocks per tile in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode. Its used logic-block area is 5.69025e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 324384, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.11978e+14. Its slowest stage is in output_logic: no multiply, the reset input pad through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 1.50 ns from the pad into the first LUT, 1.91 into the third. The next slowest stage, in output_logic, is no multiply, a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT, 6.008 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Initial values. A rewrite may not rely on any register's initial value, soft or hard: the flow drops initial values, so every register starts at an unknown value and holds one until something writes it.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone. Keep every term of the original expression, including ports every instantiation ties to a constant. A term that goes on no block port (an addend beside the products) stays outside the block, added to its result; the flow flattens the design and removes an add of a constant, so such a term puts nothing on the stage and rule 2 does not count it. A bound on a port's values may be used only if the plan states it and names its source in the design, on a line of its own beginning ASSUMES:; such a rewrite is recorded as verified under that assumption. Fold a term only when it is constant inside the module itself, and say so in the plan.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A DSP tile is in one mode, and in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode one tile holds four blocks; the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock, so blocks with different reset signals cannot share a tile and each takes a tile of its own. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Every integer DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode packs four blocks to a tile when they share one reset and one clock (the tile has one of each); blocks on different resets take a tile each.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

This mode's block holds registers

The rules above were written for blocks with no registers. This run's target mode is a registered block: its contract states a latency in cycles, where its registers sit, what its reset does and which ports are tied off. Read the rules above with the changes below.

Must hold

Register control, for this mode. The block has two register stages of its own: one on the operands, before the products, and one on the sum. A design register may go inside the block only as one of those two stages. A register on the operands, or on each product before the sum, is the first stage; the register that holds the sum is the second. The block's registers have the reset the contract states, no enable and no initial value: every one is unknown until the block's reset clears it, and the contract's reset line says when that clear lands. A design register that has an enable, a different reset behaviour, or a reset signal other than the one you connect to the block's reset port cannot be replaced by the block's register: keep it outside or leave the site alone. Connect the block's reset to the signal that reset the registers it replaces. A register had a reset only if its own RTL responds to that signal (the register is cleared or loaded when the signal is asserted); a reset port that is connected to the module but that the register never reads is not its reset, and such a register counts as one that had none. If those registers had no reset and the block must start from a defined state (a running sum, whose value is unknown until the first clear), drive the block's reset from a reset the design asserts only at start-up, and state that assumption in the plan on an ASSUMES: line (the rewrite is then verified under it); if they had no reset and the block needs no defined start (a result that depends on the last operands alone), tie the block's reset to 0.

Latency (rule 5, and it must hold). The module's latency must be unchanged: every output carries the same value on the same cycle as before. Count the register stages the original has from the operands to the register that holds the sum, and compare with the block's latency.

the original hasthen
as many stages as the blockthe block's registers stand in place of the design's; remove exactly those and move nothing else
morethe extra stage stays outside the block, on the operands before it or on the result after it, where it was
fewerthe block adds the difference: move a register that already exists on the same path into the block's place, or delay every other value that meets the block's result by the same number of cycles so that the stage adds values from the same cycle; if neither can be done without changing when the module's outputs change, leave the site alone

State in the plan the stages the original has around each site, which of them the block replaces, and the module's latency before and after. They must be equal.

Addends and the chain input. A term the original adds to the products before the sum's register cannot be added to the block's result on the same cycle: the result is already registered. The chain input is a dedicated pin that takes only another block's chain output or a constant. If the term is a module port that every instantiation ties to a constant, connect that port to the chain input: the block is then tied off exactly where the original was, and the module still computes what it computed for every value of its ports. Any other addend needs its own register (same reset) and an add after the block, which rule 2 counts on the next stage.

Weigh

Stages (rules 2 and 3). A registered block ends the stage that reaches its operand ports and starts a new one at its result. The wire into the block and the wire out of it are on different stages. Soft arithmetic after the block is on the stage after the block's result register, not in series with the block's delay. Between its own two registers the block takes its fixed delay from the table.

Flip-flops (rule 4). The design registers the block replaces leave the soft logic.

Objective: minimize the design's area x delay x wirelength product: VPR's used area times the critical path times the total routed wirelength, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.

Read the source. Output EXACTLY this header (uppercase keys), then the plan:

TIER: T1 | T2 | NONE
REGIONS: <comma-separated region id(s)> # one id for a direct swap; N ids for a fusion
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal/module the terms feed> # or NONE for a single region
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, a single integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, a single integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage your rewrite leaves, in ns, a single number>
MAPPING: <which operands map to which of the mode's ports>
PLAN: <how the module's live inputs/outputs and downstream consumers are preserved; any introduced
operations/datatypes (should be NONE for T1/T2); and the main risks>

Output rules: no preamble before the TIER line — read the source first, then write. You
are one step of an automated pipeline: a reviewer runs automatically on this plan, you
cannot invoke it, and no human reads your output, so do not end with a question or an
offer.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.v

response

I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: out_temp, the per-seq_mac running accumulator (qadd add_u1 at tpu_like.small.os.v:4697 feeding out_temp at :4700) — one reduction per seq_mac, 256 of them
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 72
STAGE_NS: 5.35
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (tpu_like.small.os.v:4666) — a port ← {1'b0, a_flopped} (9-bit zero-extension of the existing 8-bit a_flopped register, :4677/:4684); b port ← {1'b0, b_flopped} (zero-extension of b_flopped, :4678/:4685, which is in_b[7:0] of the PE); clk ← seq_mac.clk; reset ← seq_mac.reset (= effective_rst = reset | pe_reset, systolic_pe_matrix:4110); out[17:0] → only out[15:0] is used, and it drives the existing saturating down-cast at :4704-:4716 that produces the module output out. The block has no addend/term port beyond its own running sum, so nothing is tied off; a and b are both driven, no input port is left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110, feeding every PE's .reset()) is asserted only at start-up, before the first operands are applied to the array. This is needed because seq_mac's own registers (a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp) never read reset — the three always @(posedge clk) blocks at :4683, :4691, :4699 have no reset branch — so they count as registers that had none, while the block's running sum is undefined until its first clear. Driving the block's reset from effective_rst is the sanctioned route for a running sum with no design reset.

What is converted. In every one of the 256 seq_mac instances (one per processing_element / processing_element_top_edge / processing_element_bottom_edge), qmult mult_u1 (:4689, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier), qadd add_u1 (:4697, 16-bit a + b), the 16-bit product register mul_out_temp_reg (:4691-:4693) and the 16-bit accumulator register out_temp (:4699-:4701) are replaced by one mac_int_9x9 block. This is exactly the mode's expression: a single product already feeding a shared running accumulator, one accumulator per block — not products of distinct systolic elements pushed into a common sum, which the array does not have.

Registers and latency. The original has three register stages from the operands to the accumulated sum: a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1), mul_out_temp_reg (stage 2, a register on the product before the sum), out_temp (stage 3, the sum). The block supplies two. The original therefore has one stage more, so the extra one stays outside the block where it was: a_flopped/b_flopped remain as written at :4683-:4686 and feed the block's operand ports. The block's operand register stands in for mul_out_temp_reg (a register on the operands and a register on the product before the sum are the same first stage — the multiply is combinational and purely functional, so the adder input holds the same value on the same cycle), and the block's sum register stands in for out_temp. Latency from a/b to the accumulated value: 3 clock edges before, 3 after — unchanged, so out_c of every PE and matrixC* in output_logic change on exactly the cycles they changed before. The two registers taken inside have no enable, no reset branch and no initial value, so no register behaviour is deleted; a_flopped/b_flopped are untouched, and the PE's own out_a/out_b registers (:4457-:4466, which do use reset) are not touched at all.

Arithmetic exactness. i_multiplicand/i_multiplier are 8-bit unsigned wires (DWIDTH = 8, no signed anywhere in qmult/seq_mac), so a*b is an unsigned 8×8 product, max 65025, exact in 16 bits; zero-extending each to the block's 9-bit unsigned port gives the identical product (unsigned operand narrower than the port → zero-extend, allowed). qadd is a 16-bit add that wraps at 2^16; the block's sum wraps at 2^18, and out[15:0] reproduces that wrap bit for bit (narrow sum register wraps → low bits of the wider result, allowed). No product is truncated before the sum in the original, so nothing about the fused sum differs.

Observability. a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by qmult; mul_out_temp_reg only by qadd; out_temp only by qadd (feedback) and by the down-cast at :4704-:4716. Nothing that goes inside the block is read by another expression, another module, or a port. The saturating down-cast stays outside, unchanged, reading out[15:0] instead of out_temp, so seq_mac.out → processing_element.out_c → matrixC<i>_<j> → the c_data_out* capture registers in output_logic (:2331-:2346) see the identical 8-bit value on the identical cycle. Operands are exempt: in_a/in_b still exist and still feed the PE's out_a/out_b shift registers.

Blocks, tiles, stages. 256 blocks, i.e. 256 more hard-block instances than the unmodified design and 256 fewer soft multiply+add+register regions. All 256 share one clock (clk) and one reset (effective_rst), so they pack four to a tile: 64 tiles. The 32 remaining multiplies (16 in norm, 16 in activation, all ≤9 bits) stay in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode at four to a tile: 8 tiles. Total 72 of the device's 132, i.e. 16 fewer than the baseline's 88 — zero tiles on top. If the packer leaves the 32 leftovers at the two-per-tile density it used on part of the baseline, the worst case is 80 tiles, still below 88.

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. A registered block ends the stage that reaches its operand ports and starts a new one at its result, and no block's result feeds another block, so no stage carries two block delays. The three stages the rewrite creates:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped → block operands: 0.06 clock-to-Q + register-to-first-block wire 1.419–2.461 (median 1.732) = 1.79–2.52 ns.
  • Inside the block: its fixed 2.14 ns between its own two registers.
  • Block out[15:0] → down-cast soft logic → the c_data_out* capture flip-flop in output_logic. This is structurally the baseline's second-slowest stage (a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable, 6.008 ns, 2.64 ns of wire into the last LUT): the same four LUT levels remain, only their source changes from the out_temp flip-flop (0.06 clock-to-Q plus roughly 2.5 ns of wire, backing the 2.64 ns and four levels at 0.861 out of 6.008) to a DSP output pin (block into soft logic, 0.54–1.90, median 0.96). Median case ≈ 4.4 ns, worst case ≈ 5.35 ns — the number reported above. All three are below the 6.06654 ns critical path.

Effect on the objective. The rewrite does not touch the slowest stage: that is the reset input pad through four LUTs into a flip-flop in output_logic, with no multiply on it, 6.06654 ns, and the second slowest at 6.008 ns bounds any gain from it anyway. So the clock period does not improve; this conversion is paid for in tiles, and it pays back in area and wirelength. Area falls on both counts: 16 DSP tiles removed (16 × 253779 ≈ 4.06e6 of the 5.69025e7), and per PE a 16-bit soft adder plus 32 soft flip-flops (mul_out_temp_reg and out_temp) leave the clusters — 256 × ~48 soft elements, the bulk of this design's flip-flops — which should retire several hundred of the ~1239 clusters the baseline uses. Wirelength falls with them: 256 sixteen-bit accumulator feedback loops through soft adders disappear, replaced by the block's internal path. With delay flat and both area and wirelength down, the area × delay × wirelength product drops well below 1.11978e14.

Candidates left alone.

  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368/$371/$374/$377 (sop, sum_in=top) and $380 through $413 (multiply, no sum) — all 16 lanes of the same statement, :5067: the product mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var is truncated to 8 bits into variance_applied_data[i], a register with a clock enable (in_data_available || norm_in_progress) and a clear on reset || ~enable_norm (:5046-:5101). The block's registers have no enable and a different clear condition, so that register cannot be replaced, and the site has no accumulator. The twelve with no sum are isolated scalar multiplies, which do not fit a sum-of-products/MAC mode at all; the four whose sum is reported at top reach that sum only after the 8-bit truncating register, so a sum fused before truncation would differ bit for bit, and each one feeds a distinct out_data element — separate outputs, not a common reduction.
  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470 through $530 — all 16 lanes of :5479: each is one product plus a separate addend (slope_applied_data_internal[i] + data_intercept_delayed[i], :5482), and "the only product in its sum". mac_int_9x9 has no addend or chain port — its only addend is its own running sum — so the + intercept term cannot go on the block, and an isolated scalar multiply does not fit a sum-of-products mode. On top of that the product is truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal before the add, and both that register and intercept_applied_data_internal carry a clock enable (in_data_available || activation_in_progress) and a clear on reset || ~enable_activation (:5462-:5528), neither of which the block's registers provide.

Risks. (1) The effective_rst assumption above: if software pulses pe_reset between matmul tiles, the blocks clear their accumulators where the original seq_mac ignored the signal — the original accumulator is in fact never cleared and holds an unknown value for all time under this flow, so the difference is confined to a value the baseline leaves undefined, but the assumption is the thing that makes the rewrite verified. (2) The stage out of the block now starts at a DSP output pin in a DSP column rather than a flip-flop placed next to the down-cast LUTs; if placement stretches that wire past the 1.90 ns top of the sampled range, the stage I touch could approach the 6.06654 ns path and set the period. (3) Tile count after packing depends on how the 32 remaining norm/activation multiplies pack; anywhere in 72–80 it stays under the baseline's 88 and the device's 132.

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, seed 1) has a critical path of 6.06654 ns and uses 88 DSP tiles of the device's 132, four blocks per tile in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.
The block's contract, its lines on timing, registers and tie-offs:
timing: out is registered inside the block on clk; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
registers: the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
APPROVE the plan if it is correct: a legitimate adaptation does not need to be perfect, only sound.
Do NOT demand rewrites for style, minor wording, or hypothetical concerns.
REVISE ONLY for a CONCRETE, specific violation you can name: (a) it changes the computed function
when the tier forbids it; (b) it introduces an operation/datatype absent from the design and not
admitted by the tier; (c) it fabricates terms to match a mode's n_terms (products that did not
exist / do not share a reduction); (d) it wires the mode to constants or a dead sink (mode island).
A zero on an unused term port (the contract's tie-off), a zero-extension of a narrower operand,
or a constant on a port the design itself ties to that constant is not a fabricated term and
not a mode island: a block is an island only when nothing reads its result.
If none of these apply, APPROVE.
Your FIRST line must be exactly 'APPROVE' or 'REVISE', nothing else. If REVISE, the next lines
must name the specific violation (a/b/c/d) and what to change.
Output rules: no preamble before the verdict line — verify first, then write. Your
verdict is applied automatically and no human reads it, so do not end with a question
or an offer.

--- PLAN ---
I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: out_temp, the per-seq_mac running accumulator (qadd add_u1 at tpu_like.small.os.v:4697 feeding out_temp at :4700) — one reduction per seq_mac, 256 of them
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 72
STAGE_NS: 5.35
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (tpu_like.small.os.v:4666) — a port ← {1'b0, a_flopped} (9-bit zero-extension of the existing 8-bit a_flopped register, :4677/:4684); b port ← {1'b0, b_flopped} (zero-extension of b_flopped, :4678/:4685, which is in_b[7:0] of the PE); clk ← seq_mac.clk; reset ← seq_mac.reset (= effective_rst = reset | pe_reset, systolic_pe_matrix:4110); out[17:0] → only out[15:0] is used, and it drives the existing saturating down-cast at :4704-:4716 that produces the module output out. The block has no addend/term port beyond its own running sum, so nothing is tied off; a and b are both driven, no input port is left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110, feeding every PE's .reset()) is asserted only at start-up, before the first operands are applied to the array. This is needed because seq_mac's own registers (a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp) never read reset — the three always @(posedge clk) blocks at :4683, :4691, :4699 have no reset branch — so they count as registers that had none, while the block's running sum is undefined until its first clear. Driving the block's reset from effective_rst is the sanctioned route for a running sum with no design reset.

What is converted. In every one of the 256 seq_mac instances (one per processing_element / processing_element_top_edge / processing_element_bottom_edge), qmult mult_u1 (:4689, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier), qadd add_u1 (:4697, 16-bit a + b), the 16-bit product register mul_out_temp_reg (:4691-:4693) and the 16-bit accumulator register out_temp (:4699-:4701) are replaced by one mac_int_9x9 block. This is exactly the mode's expression: a single product already feeding a shared running accumulator, one accumulator per block — not products of distinct systolic elements pushed into a common sum, which the array does not have.

Registers and latency. The original has three register stages from the operands to the accumulated sum: a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1), mul_out_temp_reg (stage 2, a register on the product before the sum), out_temp (stage 3, the sum). The block supplies two. The original therefore has one stage more, so the extra one stays outside the block where it was: a_flopped/b_flopped remain as written at :4683-:4686 and feed the block's operand ports. The block's operand register stands in for mul_out_temp_reg (a register on the operands and a register on the product before the sum are the same first stage — the multiply is combinational and purely functional, so the adder input holds the same value on the same cycle), and the block's sum register stands in for out_temp. Latency from a/b to the accumulated value: 3 clock edges before, 3 after — unchanged, so out_c of every PE and matrixC* in output_logic change on exactly the cycles they changed before. The two registers taken inside have no enable, no reset branch and no initial value, so no register behaviour is deleted; a_flopped/b_flopped are untouched, and the PE's own out_a/out_b registers (:4457-:4466, which do use reset) are not touched at all.

Arithmetic exactness. i_multiplicand/i_multiplier are 8-bit unsigned wires (DWIDTH = 8, no signed anywhere in qmult/seq_mac), so a*b is an unsigned 8×8 product, max 65025, exact in 16 bits; zero-extending each to the block's 9-bit unsigned port gives the identical product (unsigned operand narrower than the port → zero-extend, allowed). qadd is a 16-bit add that wraps at 2^16; the block's sum wraps at 2^18, and out[15:0] reproduces that wrap bit for bit (narrow sum register wraps → low bits of the wider result, allowed). No product is truncated before the sum in the original, so nothing about the fused sum differs.

Observability. a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by qmult; mul_out_temp_reg only by qadd; out_temp only by qadd (feedback) and by the down-cast at :4704-:4716. Nothing that goes inside the block is read by another expression, another module, or a port. The saturating down-cast stays outside, unchanged, reading out[15:0] instead of out_temp, so seq_mac.out → processing_element.out_c → matrixC<i>_<j> → the c_data_out* capture registers in output_logic (:2331-:2346) see the identical 8-bit value on the identical cycle. Operands are exempt: in_a/in_b still exist and still feed the PE's out_a/out_b shift registers.

Blocks, tiles, stages. 256 blocks, i.e. 256 more hard-block instances than the unmodified design and 256 fewer soft multiply+add+register regions. All 256 share one clock (clk) and one reset (effective_rst), so they pack four to a tile: 64 tiles. The 32 remaining multiplies (16 in norm, 16 in activation, all ≤9 bits) stay in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode at four to a tile: 8 tiles. Total 72 of the device's 132, i.e. 16 fewer than the baseline's 88 — zero tiles on top. If the packer leaves the 32 leftovers at the two-per-tile density it used on part of the baseline, the worst case is 80 tiles, still below 88.

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. A registered block ends the stage that reaches its operand ports and starts a new one at its result, and no block's result feeds another block, so no stage carries two block delays. The three stages the rewrite creates:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped → block operands: 0.06 clock-to-Q + register-to-first-block wire 1.419–2.461 (median 1.732) = 1.79–2.52 ns.
  • Inside the block: its fixed 2.14 ns between its own two registers.
  • Block out[15:0] → down-cast soft logic → the c_data_out* capture flip-flop in output_logic. This is structurally the baseline's second-slowest stage (a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable, 6.008 ns, 2.64 ns of wire into the last LUT): the same four LUT levels remain, only their source changes from the out_temp flip-flop (0.06 clock-to-Q plus roughly 2.5 ns of wire, backing the 2.64 ns and four levels at 0.861 out of 6.008) to a DSP output pin (block into soft logic, 0.54–1.90, median 0.96). Median case ≈ 4.4 ns, worst case ≈ 5.35 ns — the number reported above. All three are below the 6.06654 ns critical path.

Effect on the objective. The rewrite does not touch the slowest stage: that is the reset input pad through four LUTs into a flip-flop in output_logic, with no multiply on it, 6.06654 ns, and the second slowest at 6.008 ns bounds any gain from it anyway. So the clock period does not improve; this conversion is paid for in tiles, and it pays back in area and wirelength. Area falls on both counts: 16 DSP tiles removed (16 × 253779 ≈ 4.06e6 of the 5.69025e7), and per PE a 16-bit soft adder plus 32 soft flip-flops (mul_out_temp_reg and out_temp) leave the clusters — 256 × ~48 soft elements, the bulk of this design's flip-flops — which should retire several hundred of the ~1239 clusters the baseline uses. Wirelength falls with them: 256 sixteen-bit accumulator feedback loops through soft adders disappear, replaced by the block's internal path. With delay flat and both area and wirelength down, the area × delay × wirelength product drops well below 1.11978e14.

Candidates left alone.

  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368/$371/$374/$377 (sop, sum_in=top) and $380 through $413 (multiply, no sum) — all 16 lanes of the same statement, :5067: the product mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var is truncated to 8 bits into variance_applied_data[i], a register with a clock enable (in_data_available || norm_in_progress) and a clear on reset || ~enable_norm (:5046-:5101). The block's registers have no enable and a different clear condition, so that register cannot be replaced, and the site has no accumulator. The twelve with no sum are isolated scalar multiplies, which do not fit a sum-of-products/MAC mode at all; the four whose sum is reported at top reach that sum only after the 8-bit truncating register, so a sum fused before truncation would differ bit for bit, and each one feeds a distinct out_data element — separate outputs, not a common reduction.
  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470 through $530 — all 16 lanes of :5479: each is one product plus a separate addend (slope_applied_data_internal[i] + data_intercept_delayed[i], :5482), and "the only product in its sum". mac_int_9x9 has no addend or chain port — its only addend is its own running sum — so the + intercept term cannot go on the block, and an isolated scalar multiply does not fit a sum-of-products mode. On top of that the product is truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal before the add, and both that register and intercept_applied_data_internal carry a clock enable (in_data_available || activation_in_progress) and a clear on reset || ~enable_activation (:5462-:5528), neither of which the block's registers provide.

Risks. (1) The effective_rst assumption above: if software pulses pe_reset between matmul tiles, the blocks clear their accumulators where the original seq_mac ignored the signal — the original accumulator is in fact never cleared and holds an unknown value for all time under this flow, so the difference is confined to a value the baseline leaves undefined, but the assumption is the thing that makes the rewrite verified. (2) The stage out of the block now starts at a DSP output pin in a DSP column rather than a flip-flop placed next to the down-cast LUTs; if placement stretches that wire past the 1.90 ns top of the sampled range, the stage I touch could approach the 6.06654 ns path and set the period. (3) Tile count after packing depends on how the 32 remaining norm/activation multiplies pack; anywhere in 72–80 it stays under the baseline's 88 and the device's 132.

response

I'll verify the plan against the source.

APPROVE

Verification against the source:

  • Region/structure — tpu_like.small.os.v:4725 is qmult's o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, instantiated at :4689 inside seq_mac (:4666). The plan's description matches the file exactly: operand registers a_flopped/b_flopped (:4683-4686), combinational multiply, product register mul_out_temp_reg (:4691), qadd (:4697) feeding out_temp (:4699), and the saturating down-cast at :4704-4716 left outside. out_temp is read only by the adder feedback and that down-cast — a real shared running accumulator, one per seq_mac.
  • (c) no fabricated terms — one product feeding its own running sum; no term port is invented and the plan explicitly notes none needs tying off. DWIDTH=8 and DESIGN_SIZE=16 confirm 256 seq_mac instances (:4455, :4551, :4648).
  • (d) not an island — out[15:0] drives the retained down-cast → out_mac → out_c (:4453) → matrixC* → output_logic capture registers. Live consumers preserved.
  • (b) no new op/datatype family — mac on int8 already present; 8-bit unsigned operands (input [DWIDTH-1:0], no signed) zero-extended to the 9-bit ports is explicitly permitted, and the 18-bit sum's low 16 bits are congruent to the original 16-bit wrapping accumulation, so the down-cast's out_temp[15] sign test is bit-identical.
  • (a) function and latency — original: 3 edges from a/b to the accumulated value (operand flops, product flop, sum flop). New: a_flopped/b_flopped kept outside, block registers operands at the next edge and the sum at the one after — still 3 edges, same values on the same cycles, since the multiply between the kept operand flop and the product flop is purely combinational.
  • Reset — seq_mac already has a reset port wired to effective_rst (:4110, every PE instance) but ignores it; the three always blocks at :4683/:4691/:4699 have no reset branch. Driving the block's mandatory reset from that existing signal is use of a design signal already on the block's interface, not a constant or dead tie, and the plan states the assumption and its risk openly. The arithmetic function is unchanged.

The remaining items (tile counts 72–80 vs. baseline 88, the 5.35 ns stage estimate, the decision to leave norm/activation multiplies alone because of their truncating, enable-and-clear registers) are engineering judgment with stated worst cases, not violations.

Edit tpu_like.small.os.v in this directory. Local restructuring: re-express the region (e.g. a multiply array + adder tree as a fused sum-of-products) to reach the mode; preserve the region's function and role, keep the rest of the design unchanged, wire the mode's real consumers.

Goal: rewrite the RTL so the hard-block mode 'mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode' is exercised in the packed netlist,
doing real work, while preserving the design's behaviour and role.
Tier: T2

Hard-block primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
computes: acc += a*b
input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
output ports: out[18]
arithmetic: the block's operands and result are unsigned. A design operand narrower than its port must be zero-extended to the port width. A signed design operand has no exact representation on this block. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
timing: out is registered inside the block on clk; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
registers: the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low; it is the only control of the running sum (no enable, no load), and every register is unknown until that first clear.
power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
Wire EVERY operand of the fused region onto the input ports (name them exactly as above); drive the region's real consumer from the output port. Tie unused term ports to 0. Do not leave input ports unconnected.

Region to adapt: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (module 'qmult', operation 'mac', datatype int8; products registered 1 stage(s) before their sum). The block is registered: its result is valid 2 cycle(s) after its operands, on the registers the contract describes. The module's latency must come out unchanged. Remove from the design exactly the register stages the approved plan says the block's own registers replace, add a delay only where the plan names one, and connect the block's clock and reset as the plan states.
Rules: edit Verilog source only; preserve the module's live inputs/outputs and its downstream
consumers; do not introduce operations or datatypes absent from the design unless this tier
admits a variant; keep the surrounding design unchanged for T1/T2. Do not fabricate terms merely
to match a mode's n_terms: a term the design did not compute is not a correct replacement.
Output rules: your edit is verified automatically and no human reads this, so do not
end with a question or an offer; state what you changed and stop.

Follow this approved plan:
I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: out_temp, the per-seq_mac running accumulator (qadd add_u1 at tpu_like.small.os.v:4697 feeding out_temp at :4700) — one reduction per seq_mac, 256 of them
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 72
STAGE_NS: 5.35
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (tpu_like.small.os.v:4666) — a port ← {1'b0, a_flopped} (9-bit zero-extension of the existing 8-bit a_flopped register, :4677/:4684); b port ← {1'b0, b_flopped} (zero-extension of b_flopped, :4678/:4685, which is in_b[7:0] of the PE); clk ← seq_mac.clk; reset ← seq_mac.reset (= effective_rst = reset | pe_reset, systolic_pe_matrix:4110); out[17:0] → only out[15:0] is used, and it drives the existing saturating down-cast at :4704-:4716 that produces the module output out. The block has no addend/term port beyond its own running sum, so nothing is tied off; a and b are both driven, no input port is left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110, feeding every PE's .reset()) is asserted only at start-up, before the first operands are applied to the array. This is needed because seq_mac's own registers (a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp) never read reset — the three always @(posedge clk) blocks at :4683, :4691, :4699 have no reset branch — so they count as registers that had none, while the block's running sum is undefined until its first clear. Driving the block's reset from effective_rst is the sanctioned route for a running sum with no design reset.

What is converted. In every one of the 256 seq_mac instances (one per processing_element / processing_element_top_edge / processing_element_bottom_edge), qmult mult_u1 (:4689, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier), qadd add_u1 (:4697, 16-bit a + b), the 16-bit product register mul_out_temp_reg (:4691-:4693) and the 16-bit accumulator register out_temp (:4699-:4701) are replaced by one mac_int_9x9 block. This is exactly the mode's expression: a single product already feeding a shared running accumulator, one accumulator per block — not products of distinct systolic elements pushed into a common sum, which the array does not have.

Registers and latency. The original has three register stages from the operands to the accumulated sum: a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1), mul_out_temp_reg (stage 2, a register on the product before the sum), out_temp (stage 3, the sum). The block supplies two. The original therefore has one stage more, so the extra one stays outside the block where it was: a_flopped/b_flopped remain as written at :4683-:4686 and feed the block's operand ports. The block's operand register stands in for mul_out_temp_reg (a register on the operands and a register on the product before the sum are the same first stage — the multiply is combinational and purely functional, so the adder input holds the same value on the same cycle), and the block's sum register stands in for out_temp. Latency from a/b to the accumulated value: 3 clock edges before, 3 after — unchanged, so out_c of every PE and matrixC* in output_logic change on exactly the cycles they changed before. The two registers taken inside have no enable, no reset branch and no initial value, so no register behaviour is deleted; a_flopped/b_flopped are untouched, and the PE's own out_a/out_b registers (:4457-:4466, which do use reset) are not touched at all.

Arithmetic exactness. i_multiplicand/i_multiplier are 8-bit unsigned wires (DWIDTH = 8, no signed anywhere in qmult/seq_mac), so a*b is an unsigned 8×8 product, max 65025, exact in 16 bits; zero-extending each to the block's 9-bit unsigned port gives the identical product (unsigned operand narrower than the port → zero-extend, allowed). qadd is a 16-bit add that wraps at 2^16; the block's sum wraps at 2^18, and out[15:0] reproduces that wrap bit for bit (narrow sum register wraps → low bits of the wider result, allowed). No product is truncated before the sum in the original, so nothing about the fused sum differs.

Observability. a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by qmult; mul_out_temp_reg only by qadd; out_temp only by qadd (feedback) and by the down-cast at :4704-:4716. Nothing that goes inside the block is read by another expression, another module, or a port. The saturating down-cast stays outside, unchanged, reading out[15:0] instead of out_temp, so seq_mac.out → processing_element.out_c → matrixC<i>_<j> → the c_data_out* capture registers in output_logic (:2331-:2346) see the identical 8-bit value on the identical cycle. Operands are exempt: in_a/in_b still exist and still feed the PE's out_a/out_b shift registers.

Blocks, tiles, stages. 256 blocks, i.e. 256 more hard-block instances than the unmodified design and 256 fewer soft multiply+add+register regions. All 256 share one clock (clk) and one reset (effective_rst), so they pack four to a tile: 64 tiles. The 32 remaining multiplies (16 in norm, 16 in activation, all ≤9 bits) stay in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode at four to a tile: 8 tiles. Total 72 of the device's 132, i.e. 16 fewer than the baseline's 88 — zero tiles on top. If the packer leaves the 32 leftovers at the two-per-tile density it used on part of the baseline, the worst case is 80 tiles, still below 88.

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. A registered block ends the stage that reaches its operand ports and starts a new one at its result, and no block's result feeds another block, so no stage carries two block delays. The three stages the rewrite creates:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped → block operands: 0.06 clock-to-Q + register-to-first-block wire 1.419–2.461 (median 1.732) = 1.79–2.52 ns.
  • Inside the block: its fixed 2.14 ns between its own two registers.
  • Block out[15:0] → down-cast soft logic → the c_data_out* capture flip-flop in output_logic. This is structurally the baseline's second-slowest stage (a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable, 6.008 ns, 2.64 ns of wire into the last LUT): the same four LUT levels remain, only their source changes from the out_temp flip-flop (0.06 clock-to-Q plus roughly 2.5 ns of wire, backing the 2.64 ns and four levels at 0.861 out of 6.008) to a DSP output pin (block into soft logic, 0.54–1.90, median 0.96). Median case ≈ 4.4 ns, worst case ≈ 5.35 ns — the number reported above. All three are below the 6.06654 ns critical path.

Effect on the objective. The rewrite does not touch the slowest stage: that is the reset input pad through four LUTs into a flip-flop in output_logic, with no multiply on it, 6.06654 ns, and the second slowest at 6.008 ns bounds any gain from it anyway. So the clock period does not improve; this conversion is paid for in tiles, and it pays back in area and wirelength. Area falls on both counts: 16 DSP tiles removed (16 × 253779 ≈ 4.06e6 of the 5.69025e7), and per PE a 16-bit soft adder plus 32 soft flip-flops (mul_out_temp_reg and out_temp) leave the clusters — 256 × ~48 soft elements, the bulk of this design's flip-flops — which should retire several hundred of the ~1239 clusters the baseline uses. Wirelength falls with them: 256 sixteen-bit accumulator feedback loops through soft adders disappear, replaced by the block's internal path. With delay flat and both area and wirelength down, the area × delay × wirelength product drops well below 1.11978e14.

Candidates left alone.

  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368/$371/$374/$377 (sop, sum_in=top) and $380 through $413 (multiply, no sum) — all 16 lanes of the same statement, :5067: the product mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var is truncated to 8 bits into variance_applied_data[i], a register with a clock enable (in_data_available || norm_in_progress) and a clear on reset || ~enable_norm (:5046-:5101). The block's registers have no enable and a different clear condition, so that register cannot be replaced, and the site has no accumulator. The twelve with no sum are isolated scalar multiplies, which do not fit a sum-of-products/MAC mode at all; the four whose sum is reported at top reach that sum only after the 8-bit truncating register, so a sum fused before truncation would differ bit for bit, and each one feeds a distinct out_data element — separate outputs, not a common reduction.
  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470 through $530 — all 16 lanes of :5479: each is one product plus a separate addend (slope_applied_data_internal[i] + data_intercept_delayed[i], :5482), and "the only product in its sum". mac_int_9x9 has no addend or chain port — its only addend is its own running sum — so the + intercept term cannot go on the block, and an isolated scalar multiply does not fit a sum-of-products mode. On top of that the product is truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal before the add, and both that register and intercept_applied_data_internal carry a clock enable (in_data_available || activation_in_progress) and a clear on reset || ~enable_activation (:5462-:5528), neither of which the block's registers provide.

Risks. (1) The effective_rst assumption above: if software pulses pe_reset between matmul tiles, the blocks clear their accumulators where the original seq_mac ignored the signal — the original accumulator is in fact never cleared and holds an unknown value for all time under this flow, so the difference is confined to a value the baseline leaves undefined, but the assumption is the thing that makes the rewrite verified. (2) The stage out of the block now starts at a DSP output pin in a DSP column rather than a flip-flop placed next to the down-cast LUTs; if placement stretches that wire past the 1.90 ns top of the sampled range, the stage I touch could approach the 6.06654 ns path and set the period. (3) Tile count after packing depends on how the 32 remaining norm/activation multiplies pack; anywhere in 72–80 it stays under the baseline's 88 and the device's 132.

module seq_mac(a, b, out, reset, clk);
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
input reset;
input clk;
output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_temp;

reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;

wire [17:0] mac_out;

always @(posedge clk) begin
    a_flopped <= a;
    b_flopped <= b;
end

//The multiply, the product register, the accumulating add and the accumulator
//register are all fused into one hard MAC block. The block registers its
//operands at one clock edge and its running sum at the next, so the latency
//from a_flopped/b_flopped to the accumulated value is the same two stages
//that mul_out_temp_reg and out_temp used to provide.
//Operands are unsigned 8-bit, so they are zero-extended to the block's 9-bit
//ports. The block's sum wraps at 2^18; bits [15:0] are the same bits the
//16-bit accumulator used to hold.
mac_int_9x9 mac_u1(
    .clk(clk),
    .reset(reset),
    .a({1'b0, a_flopped}),
    .b({1'b0, b_flopped}),
    .out(mac_out)
);

//we just truncate the higher bits of the product
assign out_temp = mac_out[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

//down cast the result
assign out = 
    (out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] == 0) ?  //positive number
        (
           (|(out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 1, that means overlfow
             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : //sign bit and then all 1s
             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , out_temp[`DWIDTH-2:0]} 
        )
        : //negative number
        (
           (|(out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 0, that means overlfow
             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , out_temp[`DWIDTH-2:0]} :
             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}} //sign bit and then all 0s
        );

endmodule

//Hard MAC block: unsigned 9x9 multiply accumulated into an 18-bit running sum.
//Operands are registered at one clock edge, the sum at the next. reset is
//synchronous and active high and clears every register of the block; it is the
//only control of the running sum (no enable, no load).
(* blackbox *)
module mac_int_9x9(clk, reset, a, b, out);

response

I'll examine the file before judging.

ACCEPT

Role preserved (witness → after). The witness records role qmult, operation mac, datatype int8, region qmult.$mul$...$349(sum_in=seq_mac), downstream consumer qmult/seq_mac. That is exactly one 8×8 product feeding the seq_mac running sum inside every systolic PE. After the edit, seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4665) still performs operand-flop → multiply → accumulate → the same saturating down-cast at lines 4704‑4716. The arithmetic role is a datapath MAC in an output-stationary systolic PE both before and after — no control-path repurposing, so no (c).

Real region, real reduction — not fabricated. The fused mode is a single 9×9 multiply plus a cycle-to-cycle accumulate, matching the witness's (single region) reduction. No multi-term dot product was synthesized: the four things folded in (qmult multiply, mul_out_temp_reg, qadd, out_temp register) are precisely the four elements that already formed this MAC's recurrence in the original. The running sum over successive cycles is genuinely the reduction the original accumulator performed, so no (a).

  • Operands: qmult was an unsigned Verilog * on 8-bit wires, so zero-extending to the 9-bit ports is bit-faithful; products (≤65025) fit in 16 bits.
  • Accumulator: out_temp = mac_out[15:0] — the low 16 bits of an 18-bit running sum equal the original mod-2¹⁶ 16-bit accumulation, bit-exact into the unchanged down-cast.
  • Latency: original was a→a_flopped(1), product reg(2), out_temp(3). New is a_flopped(1) + the block's two stated register stages = 3. Throughput one product per edge, matching the contract's "no enable, adds one product every clock edge that does not clear it."

Output actually flows (no mode island). out_mac → out_c (:4452) → matrixC* at each PE instantiation (:4111, :4368) → c_data_out / c_data_out_15 (:2330, :2345), the matmul's real result bus. Nothing is tied off or sunk; a_flopped/b_flopped remain live drivers of the block. The only control, reset, is bound to the PE's effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (:4109) — the array's per-matrix accumulator clear, which is the semantically correct consumer of an OS PE accumulator clear, not a constant. So no (b).

Hygiene. qmult/qadd have no remaining instantiations anywhere in the file (only the updated hierarchy comment references the new block); no dangling nets left behind.

(d) not applicable: T2, evidence floor records no precision/algorithm variant introduced, and the manifest introduces no new operations or datatypes — the design stays int8 fixed-point matmul, the workload character the suite already contains.

4665	module seq_mac(a, b, out, reset, clk);
4666	input [`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
4667	input [`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
4668	input reset;
4669	input clk;
4670	output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;
4671	
4672	wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_temp;
4673	
4674	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
4675	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;
4676	
4677	wire [17:0] mac_out;
4678	
4679	always @(posedge clk) begin
4680	    a_flopped <= a;
4681	    b_flopped <= b;
4682	end
4683	
4684	//The multiply, the product register, the accumulating add and the accumulator
4685	//register are all fused into one hard MAC block. The block registers its
4686	//operands at one clock edge and its running sum at the next, so the latency
4687	//from a_flopped/b_flopped to the accumulated value is the same two stages
4688	//that mul_out_temp_reg and out_temp used to provide.
4689	//Operands are unsigned 8-bit, so they are zero-extended to the block's 9-bit
4690	//ports. The block's sum wraps at 2^18; bits [15:0] are the same bits the
4691	//16-bit accumulator used to hold.
4692	mac_int_9x9 mac_u1(
4693	    .clk(clk),
4694	    .reset(reset),
4695	    .a({1'b0, a_flopped}),
4696	    .b({1'b0, b_flopped}),
4697	    .out(mac_out)
4698	);
4699	
4700	//we just truncate the higher bits of the product
4701	assign out_temp = mac_out[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];
4702	
4703	//down cast the result
4704	assign out = 
4705	    (out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] == 0) ?  //positive number
4706	        (
4707	           (|(out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 1, that means overlfow
4708	             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : //sign bit and then all 1s
4709	             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , out_temp[`DWIDTH-2:0]} 
4710	        )
4711	        : //negative number
4712	        (
4713	           (|(out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 0, that means overlfow
4714	             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , out_temp[`DWIDTH-2:0]} :
4715	             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}} //sign bit and then all 0s
4716	        );
4717	
4718	endmodule
4719	
4720	//Hard MAC block: unsigned 9x9 multiply accumulated into an 18-bit running sum.
4721	//Operands are registered at one clock edge, the sum at the next. reset is
4722	//synchronous and active high and clears every register of the block; it is the
4723	//only control of the running sum (no enable, no load).
4724	(* blackbox *)
4725	module mac_int_9x9(clk, reset, a, b, out);
4726	input clk;
4727	input reset;
4728	input [8:0] a;
4729	input [8:0] b;
4730	output [17:0] out;
4731	endmodule
4732

'mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode' (Hard-block primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
computes: acc += a*b
input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
output ports: out[18]
arithmetic: the block's operands and result are unsigned. A design operand narrower than its port must be zero-extended to the port width. A signed design operand has no exact representation on this block. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
timing: out is registered inside the block on clk; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
registers: the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low; it is the only control of the running sum (no enable, no load), and every register is unknown until that first clear.
power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.

qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) | mac | 256 | on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 56 of the 88 tiles (the 256 element products among them; the split by family is not on file) | products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum | the only product in its sum
An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, seed 1) has a critical path of 6.06654 ns and uses 88 DSP tiles of the device's 132, four blocks per tile in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode. Its used logic-block area is 5.69025e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 324384, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.11978e+14. Its slowest stage is in output_logic: no multiply, the reset input pad through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 1.50 ns from the pad into the first LUT, 1.91 into the third. The next slowest stage, in output_logic, is no multiply, a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT, 6.008 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Initial values. A rewrite may not rely on any register's initial value, soft or hard: the flow drops initial values, so every register starts at an unknown value and holds one until something writes it.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone. Keep every term of the original expression, including ports every instantiation ties to a constant. A term that goes on no block port (an addend beside the products) stays outside the block, added to its result; the flow flattens the design and removes an add of a constant, so such a term puts nothing on the stage and rule 2 does not count it. A bound on a port's values may be used only if the plan states it and names its source in the design, on a line of its own beginning ASSUMES:; such a rewrite is recorded as verified under that assumption. Fold a term only when it is constant inside the module itself, and say so in the plan.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A DSP tile is in one mode, and in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode one tile holds four blocks; the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock, so blocks with different reset signals cannot share a tile and each takes a tile of its own. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Measured on this architecture

Block delay, clock-to-Q and blocks per tile are from the arch file; wire and adder delays are sampled, a range with the median in parentheses. A row marked "on this design" is measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, from the paths its timing report lists, with the design's hard multiplier standing for the block; the other wire and adder rows are sampled over earlier runs of other designs on this architecture.

Every integer DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode packs four blocks to a tile when they share one reset and one clock (the tile has one of each); blocks on different resets take a tile each.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

This mode's block holds registers

The rules above were written for blocks with no registers. This run's target mode is a registered block: its contract states a latency in cycles, where its registers sit, what its reset does and which ports are tied off. Read the rules above with the changes below.

Must hold

Register control, for this mode. The block has two register stages of its own: one on the operands, before the products, and one on the sum. A design register may go inside the block only as one of those two stages. A register on the operands, or on each product before the sum, is the first stage; the register that holds the sum is the second. The block's registers have the reset the contract states, no enable and no initial value: every one is unknown until the block's reset clears it, and the contract's reset line says when that clear lands. A design register that has an enable, a different reset behaviour, or a reset signal other than the one you connect to the block's reset port cannot be replaced by the block's register: keep it outside or leave the site alone. Connect the block's reset to the signal that reset the registers it replaces. A register had a reset only if its own RTL responds to that signal (the register is cleared or loaded when the signal is asserted); a reset port that is connected to the module but that the register never reads is not its reset, and such a register counts as one that had none. If those registers had no reset and the block must start from a defined state (a running sum, whose value is unknown until the first clear), drive the block's reset from a reset the design asserts only at start-up, and state that assumption in the plan on an ASSUMES: line (the rewrite is then verified under it); if they had no reset and the block needs no defined start (a result that depends on the last operands alone), tie the block's reset to 0.

Latency (rule 5, and it must hold). The module's latency must be unchanged: every output carries the same value on the same cycle as before. Count the register stages the original has from the operands to the register that holds the sum, and compare with the block's latency.

the original hasthen
as many stages as the blockthe block's registers stand in place of the design's; remove exactly those and move nothing else
morethe extra stage stays outside the block, on the operands before it or on the result after it, where it was
fewerthe block adds the difference: move a register that already exists on the same path into the block's place, or delay every other value that meets the block's result by the same number of cycles so that the stage adds values from the same cycle; if neither can be done without changing when the module's outputs change, leave the site alone

State in the plan the stages the original has around each site, which of them the block replaces, and the module's latency before and after. They must be equal.

Addends and the chain input. A term the original adds to the products before the sum's register cannot be added to the block's result on the same cycle: the result is already registered. The chain input is a dedicated pin that takes only another block's chain output or a constant. If the term is a module port that every instantiation ties to a constant, connect that port to the chain input: the block is then tied off exactly where the original was, and the module still computes what it computed for every value of its ports. Any other addend needs its own register (same reset) and an add after the block, which rule 2 counts on the next stage.

Weigh

Stages (rules 2 and 3). A registered block ends the stage that reaches its operand ports and starts a new one at its result. The wire into the block and the wire out of it are on different stages. Soft arithmetic after the block is on the stage after the block's result register, not in series with the block's delay. Between its own two registers the block takes its fixed delay from the table.

Flip-flops (rule 4). The design registers the block replaces leave the soft logic.

Objective: minimize the design's area x delay x wirelength product: VPR's used area times the critical path times the total routed wirelength, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.
